2026-09-19  R-484  Backend — real-time build streaming (SSE). First of the founder's post-roadmap priorities ("streaming first, then scope isolation properly"), chosen after three parallel research passes covering competitor streaming architecture, per-user isolation scoping, and deploy/stack breadth. Replaces the one-shot blocking "Building…" wait with genuine incremental progress; scoped as backend only — console UI consumption is R-485, a fast-follow, mirroring this session's proven backend-then-UI split (R-476→R-477, R-478→R-479). Design verified by direct source read before writing code: `model_gateway/contracts.py`'s `ModelProvider` Protocol already declares `stream(request) -> AsyncIterator[StreamEvent]` and every real provider already implements it, but `_build()`'s entire call chain (`build_app_from_prompt` → `generate_ir` → `provider.generate()`) never once called it — the gap this task closes is entirely in the intake/build layer, not the model layer. Added purely additive streaming twins, leaving every existing non-streaming function untouched: `generate_ir_stream()` (`intake/nl_to_ir.py`, an async generator yielding raw text deltas from `provider.stream()` then the final `IntakeResult`), `build_app_from_prompt_stream()` (`intake/build_app.py`, streams the IR-generation deltas then does the existing `build_app_from_ir()`'s disk/git work synchronously once the IR completes), and `_build_stream()` (`studio/live_serve.py`, an async generator mirroring `_build()`'s plain-prompt-only branch — Solution Pack, Ecosystem, and `hybrid_ui=True` builds are explicitly, honestly rejected via a new `StreamingBuildNotSupportedError` rather than silently falling back or guessing, matching the exact scope precedent `_edit()`/R-476 already set). New `POST /api/build/stream` route in `studio/server.py`: rejects unsupported build kinds with a normal `400` before any streaming starts, then switches to Server-Sent Events (`Content-Type: text/event-stream`, manual `self.wfile.write()`+`flush()` per event over stdlib `http.server`'s connection-close-delimited body, zero new dependencies), driven via a small `asyncio.run()`-wrapped drain loop matching `_edit()`'s existing "sync handler, async model calls" pattern. New Go control-plane route `POST /jobs/build/stream` (`internal/jobs/handler.go`): because crediting requires the final event's usage (only known once the stream completes), the relay cannot inject `credits_spent`/`credit_balance` into an already-sent frame — instead it relays every upstream SSE frame verbatim via `http.Flusher` as it arrives (a new `readSSEFrame`/`parseSSEDataPayload` pair reads exactly one frame at a time without altering what gets forwarded), then appends one trailing `event: credits\ndata: {...}\n\n` frame once the upstream `"done"` event's usage is known and debited via the same `creditsForUsage` computation `proxyAndDebit` already uses — including the edge case where `"done"` carries no `usage` at all (still emits a `credits_spent: 0` frame, matching the non-streaming response's own always-present shape, distinguished internally via a separate `sawDone` boolean rather than conflating "no usage" with "stream never completed"); a debit failure after the stream has already started (headers already sent, so a plain `500` is no longer possible) surfaces as a named `credits_error` SSE frame instead, an honest partial-failure shape. **Two real bugs found and fixed by this task's own gates, not glossed over**: (1) the SSE route initially sent `Connection: keep-alive`, which `BaseHTTPRequestHandler.send_header` specially interprets as "keep this socket open" (`self.close_connection = False`) — since the response has no `Content-Length`/chunked framing, the client's only signal the body has ended is the connection closing, so this hung every real client indefinitely; caught immediately by the first HTTP-level streaming test via a real client socket timeout, fixed by sending `Connection: close` instead. (2) `RecordingProvider` (`model_gateway/recording.py`) — the real usage/cost-tracking wrapper that every production build call is routed through via `resolve_generation_provider_from_env` — had no `.stream()` method at all; every automated test mocks around this real wrapping (handing `_build_stream()` a raw stub provider directly), so this was invisible to `task verify` and only surfaced via the task's own required live `curl -N` smoke test, which failed immediately with `'RecordingProvider' object has no attribute 'stream'`; fixed by adding `RecordingProvider.stream()`, mirroring `generate()`'s own success/failure ledger-recording shape exactly, plus 4 new tests in `test_recording_provider.py` (both files added to the task's `allowed_paths` mid-task once the gap was found, following the same discipline as every prior mid-task scope correction this session). agent-engine `task verify`: **3,658 tests OK** (27 net-new: 9 in `test_intake_nl_to_ir.py`, 3 in `test_build_app.py`, 11 in `test_studio_server.py`, 4 in `test_recording_provider.py`), 0 model/network calls — every new function exercised only against in-memory stub/stream providers. Control-plane `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./...`: all green, 7 new tests in `handler_test.go` (55 total), covering verbatim frame relay + real debit after `done`, a `credits` frame still appended (charging 0) when `done` carries no usage, a mid-stream `error` frame relayed with no trailing `credits` frame, pre-stream Solution Pack/Ecosystem/`hybrid_ui` rejections proxied unchanged, upstream-unreachable → 502, and a debit failure surfacing as `credits_error`. Repo-wide `task verify`/`lint`/`security:quick`/`env:check` and a new `scripts/test.sh` contract block all pass. Live manual smoke against the real Docker control-plane (rebuilt via `task control-plane:verify`) and a freshly restarted real agent-engine Studio server (Python doesn't hot-reload — the first attempt against the still-running pre-fix process is exactly what surfaced bug #2 above): a real `curl -N` session through the real Go control-plane (not directly against the agent-engine) showed genuine token-by-token `generating_ir` deltas arriving with real millisecond-scale gaps over ~9 real seconds of wall-clock time, ending with a real `"phase": "done"` frame carrying the full real build result (177 files, entities `Tag, TaskComment, TaskItem, TaskTag, TodoList, User`, a real git commit sha, real `usage` with `input_tokens: 1875, output_tokens: 3144`), followed by a real trailing `event: credits` frame (`credit_balance: 100, credits_spent: 0` — honestly zero because this environment's configured cloud model has no price-book entry, a pre-existing, unrelated fact already documented in R-472/R-476's own evidence); all three unsupported build kinds (`pack_id`, `ecosystem_id`, `hybrid_ui`) confirmed cleanly rejected with a `400` and `Content-Type: application/json` before any SSE framing began. The existing non-streaming `POST /jobs/build`/`POST /api/build` were not touched by any edit in this task and remain covered unchanged by every pre-existing passing test. NEXT: R-485 (console: streaming build UI, consuming this endpoint in `studio-chat.tsx`'s `sendBuild()`), then properly scope (not yet build) full per-user process/sandbox isolation per the founder's stated build order.

2026-09-19  R-483  Fix — dynamic-route slug collision & duplicate FK identifier in the edit-delta path. Second follow-up task after the 7-task Phase D roadmap shipped, per the founder's "complete one by one all" direction. Fixes a real, reproducible bug found live during R-481's own manual smoke test (documented in `.ai/tasks/R-481.md`'s Gates & Evidence): (1) a Next.js dev-server crash (`Error: You cannot use different slug names for the same dynamic path ('counterId' !== 'counter_id')`) caused by a follow-up edit producing a new endpoint whose `{param}` path segment was spelled differently than an existing endpoint's own `{param}` for the same resource; (2) a duplicate `lib/types.ts` identifier (`TS2300`/`TS2687`/`TS2717`) caused by an edit's proposed entity declaring both an explicit field and a same-named to-one relation for the same conceptual foreign key. Root cause verified by direct source investigation before writing any fix, not assumed: the full-build prompt (`intake/nl_to_ir.py`) has no explicit casing rule for API path `{param}`s (camelCase only by its own worked example in `application_ir/examples.py`), while the edit-delta prompt (`intake/app_delta.py`) explicitly told the model to use `lower_snake_case` for the *entire* path including `{param}` placeholders — nothing in `parse_app_delta_proposal` or `apply_app_delta` reconciled a new endpoint's param spelling against an existing endpoint's own param for the same resource (`apply_app_delta` never even calls `normalize_ir()`, confirmed only the full-build path does). Separately, `codegen/nextjs.py`'s `_entity_interface()` (the `lib/types.ts` generator) computes a `declared` set of existing field names but only ever consults it for `created_at`/`updated_at`/`created_by` — for every to-one relation it unconditionally appended `f"{relation.name}_id"` with no check against `declared`; `Entity.__post_init__` validates field-name uniqueness and relation-name uniqueness as two separate sets, never cross-checking that a relation's implied FK column doesn't collide with an already-declared field. Confirmed this exact shape (an entity with both an explicit `post_id`-style field and a same-named relation) already sat, unexercised for this, in `tests/test_app_delta.py`'s own `_VALID_DELTA` fixture — both defects are deterministic, systemic code/prompt facts, not one-off model randomness, and reproduce reliably for any entity/resource name. Fixed at the structural root, not just the two known call sites: `ApiEndpoint.__post_init__` (`application_ir/ir.py`) now canonicalizes every `{param}` to camelCase unconditionally via a new pure, idempotent `_canonicalize_path_params()` helper, applied at the one place every `ApiEndpoint` is ever constructed — fresh build, edit delta, solution packs, ecosystem packs, and any future caller — closing the bug class structurally. Both prompts also corrected to ask for camelCase `{param}`s and explicit reuse of an existing resource's param name, as a first line of defense (the code-level fix is the real guarantee, not reliant on the model following instructions). `_entity_interface()` now skips the synthesized `<relation>_id` field when a same-named field is already explicitly declared — the explicit field's own declaration always wins. A pre-existing test (`test_form_update_screens.py`'s `test_nextjs_web_adapter_generates_complete_project` and `test_subcollection_view_renders_new_child_link`) initially failed after the fix — investigated, not reverted: its fixture's own hardcoded `ApiEndpoint` path used the raw `{article_id}` spelling, and the previously-passing assertion matched that exact raw spelling in the generated query-string link. Confirmed this was never a functional dependency on snake_case specifically: the generated reader code (`comment_editor/page.tsx`) already defensively checks THREE possible spellings (`article_id`, then `articleId`, then `article`), with `articleId` as its own second-priority fallback — clear pre-existing evidence this exact inconsistency was already anticipated by the generator's own reader code. Updated both assertions to expect the new, correct, canonical `articleId=` camelCase output rather than reverting the fix; the fixture's own raw `{article_id}` literal was deliberately left unchanged so the test continues to exercise the real canonicalization end-to-end. Six new regression tests added: `ApiPathParamCanonicalizationTests` (5 cases in `test_application_ir.py`, including one reproducing the exact live bug scenario) and `DuplicateForeignKeyFieldTests` (1 case in `test_nextjs_adapter.py`, asserting the generated `lib/types.ts` contains the FK identifier exactly once). Gates: agent-engine `task verify` — **Ran 3,635 tests, OK** (6 new); repo `task verify`/`lint`/`security:quick`/`env:check` all pass. Live (real Docker control-plane already running, a freshly restarted real agent-engine Studio server in preview mode with the fix, real console already running) — **reproduced the exact originally-reported scenario end to end**: `POST /jobs/build` with the identical original prompt ("A simple counter app where users track a single number that goes up or down") → real 157-file build; `POST /jobs/build/1/edit` with the identical original follow-up prompt ("add a reset button that sets the counter back to zero") → real second commit; `POST /jobs/build/1/preview` → real `200 {"status":"ready",...}`, **no crash this time** — confirmed by grepping the agent-engine's own real log for the exact original error string (`"different slug names"` / `"Failed to reload dynamic routes"`), genuinely absent; listed the real generated app's dynamic route directories on disk → exactly **one** (`apps/web/app/counters/[counterId]`), no colliding sibling `[counter_id]` folder; `POST /jobs/build/1/problems` → a real `tsc` run reported 8 real errors, **zero of them duplicate-identifier errors (no TS2300/TS2687/TS2717) and zero of them in `lib/types.ts`** — the 8 remaining are pre-existing, unrelated LLM-synthesized-UI issues in `app/page.tsx` (the same class already honestly documented as out-of-scope noise in R-480's/R-481's own live smokes, not this task's concern); read the real generated `lib/types.ts` directly → the `Counter` interface has exactly one `user_id?: string;` line, no duplicate — confirmed the fix live, not just via the compiler. NEXT (per the founder's "complete one by one all"): R-484 (real-time build/edit streaming, renumbered from R-483 now that this bug fix took that number), per-user backend multi-tenancy, and Publish/deploy each involve a materially different architecture decision (transport protocol, tenancy model, deploy target) requiring explicit founder direction before implementation, per the standing "stop and ask for hard-gate architecture decisions" rule.

2026-09-19  R-482  Model Provider settings UI. First follow-up task after the 7-task Phase D roadmap shipped, per the founder's "complete one by one all" direction (2026-09-19): continue through every named follow-up, one Tracker ID at a time, full discipline. New, never surfaced anywhere before: `intake/provider_resolution.py`'s `resolve_generation_provider_from_env()` always succeeds (it falls through to local Ollama at the end, never raises for "nothing configured") and its returned `ModelProvider` has a real `.provider_id` property — calling it is a safe, side-effect-free way (aside from one real but lightweight ≤1.5s Ollama reachability ping) to report exactly which provider would run the *next* real build/edit right now. New `_provider_status()` in `studio/live_serve.py` returns `platform_overview()`'s dict augmented with `activeNow: {providerId, modelId} | null` and `activeNowError: string | null`. New zero-arg `GET /api/providers` route in `studio/server.py` (new `ProvidersFn` type, new `providers_fn` param, mirroring the existing zero-arg `/api/preview` route's dispatch shape) — wired into `live_serve.py`'s always-on `control_kwargs` (build-only mode included: needs only env vars and one optional local ping, never a toolchain or running preview, the same reasoning already established for `edit_fn`/`turns_fn`/`problems_check_fn`). New control-plane route `GET /jobs/providers`, auth-required, **no credit debit** (a status read, not a model call), plain `proxyGet`. New console additions: `lib/control-plane.ts`'s `ProviderStatus`/`ProviderInfo` types + `getProviderStatus()`, new proxy route `app/api/providers/route.ts`, new authenticated `app/settings/layout.tsx` + `app/settings/page.tsx` (auth gate mirrors `app/studio/layout.tsx`'s exact pattern), a new `SettingsIcon` (gear) in `studio-icons.tsx`, linked from the Studio topbar (a new icon-button next to `LogoutButton`) and the home page's existing link list. The Settings page shows a live "Ready"/"Not ready" active-now callout at the top, then the same Provider table shape `/fabric` already established (`badge on/off` for Active/Needs key) — reusing the established visual language, now live-polled instead of a static snapshot; `/fabric` itself, `data/overview.json`, and `task console:snapshot` are all untouched, a deliberately separate, existing surface. **A real bug was found AND FIXED during this task's own live smoke test, introduced by this task's own first draft — not a pre-existing platform issue**: calling `platform_overview()` *before* `resolve_generation_provider_from_env()` meant the providers list's `active` flags were computed from `os.environ` *before* `.env` had ever been lazily loaded into the process (a side effect of `resolve_generation_provider_from_env()`'s own `load_dotenv=True` default) — so in a fresh process that had never yet handled a real build, every cloud provider showed "Needs key" even when a real key was configured, while `activeNow` (computed second, after the dotenv load already happened as a side effect) correctly named the real provider. An honest, internally self-contradictory response, not a crash — caught only because the live smoke test used a freshly started agent-engine process, exactly the scenario a real first-time user would hit. Fixed by resolving `activeNow` first, then `platform_overview()` second, so both parts of one response see the same, fully-loaded environment — verified with `env -i` (a completely clean environment, guaranteeing no accidental prior dotenv load) both before (reproducing the bug: `groq`/`google-gemini` showed `active: false` despite real configured keys) and after (confirming the fix: both correctly `active: true`) the change. Gates: agent-engine `task verify` — **3,629 OK** (4 new tests: get/404-disabled/502-on-error/available-without-preview-wiring); control-plane `go build`/`vet`/`test` all green (48 tests, 3 new: providers auth/proxy-verbatim-no-debit/unreachable); console `typecheck`/`lint`/`build` all clean (20 routes, 2 new: `/api/providers`, `/settings`); repo `task verify`/`lint`/`security:quick`/`env:check` all pass. Live (real Docker control-plane rebuilt via `control-plane.sh verify`, real agent-engine Studio server in preview mode, freshly restarted after the fix): real `GET /api/providers` (via the console proxy) returned `activeNow: {"providerId":"groq","modelId":"openai/gpt-oss-120b"}`, 11 providers listed, `groq`/`google-gemini`/`ollama-local` all correctly `active: true`; real `GET /settings` rendered the live "Ready" callout and the correct provider table. **Cross-verified `activeNow` against a real build, not just its own internal consistency**: `POST /api/jobs/build` with "A simple todo app" produced a real build whose own agent-engine log showed real Groq rate-limit retries (`groq is rate-limited (429); waiting...`) — confirming the real call genuinely went to Groq, exactly matching `activeNow`'s own report. Unauthenticated `GET /api/providers` → `401`; unauthenticated `GET /settings` → `307` redirect to `/login`, same as `/studio`'s own auth gate. NEXT (per the founder's "complete one by one all"): scope and fix the dynamic-route slug-collision codegen bug found live during R-481's own smoke test (a Next.js dynamic-route slug-name collision, "counterId" vs "counter_id", introduced by the edit-delta path). After that, R-483 (real-time build/edit streaming), per-user backend multi-tenancy, and Publish/deploy each involve a materially different architecture decision (transport protocol, tenancy model, deploy target) requiring explicit founder direction before implementation, per the standing "stop and ask for hard-gate architecture decisions" rule.

2026-09-19  R-480  Backend — Problems/compile-report support. Sixth of the founder-approved 7-task plan (R-475–R-481). Real compile-error reporting for the first time in this codebase — the founder's explicit choice over a placeholder, even though it made the roadmap longer. Confirmed by exhaustive grep during the original planning research: `verify/compile.py` has a real `CompileError`/`CompileReport`/`compile_web_project()`, but its only caller anywhere in the codebase was a standalone CLI script — never the Studio's own `_build()`/`_edit()` path. Deliberately **on-demand, not automatic**: `node_modules`/`tsc` only exist in a generated repo after a live-preview install (confirmed by this session's own R-478/R-479 smoke tests, which needed a real `pnpm install` before any Node toolchain existed) — automating a compile check on every build/edit would make the fast, network-light core loop slower and more toolchain-dependent, a real regression this task avoids. New `services/agent-engine/.../studio/problems.py` mirrors `files.py`'s shape exactly: `check_build_problems(root_dir, runner=None)` resolves `root_dir/apps/web` (the same convention `localrun/plan.py`'s `build_run_plan` already uses to detect a web target), raises `NoWebTargetError` if there's no `package.json` there, calls the real `compile_web_project()`, and remaps its `VerifyError` (raised when `node_modules/.bin/tsc` doesn't exist) into a clear, actionable `ToolchainNotInstalledError` — never silently installs dependencies as a side effect of a "check problems" click. `runner` passes straight through to `compile_web_project`, mirroring `verify/compile.py`'s own test style, so this module's own tests need no real toolchain. `StudioProblemsStore` is a bounded, thread-safe, in-memory per-build-id LRU cache mirroring `StudioSessionStore`'s own idiom, so a repeated `GET` doesn't have to recompile. New agent-engine routes `POST`/`GET /api/build/{id}/problems` (mirroring the existing `_build_id_for_suffix` + handler pattern used for `/files`/`/file`/`/turns`/`/edit`), wired into `live_serve.py`'s always-on `control_kwargs` (build-only mode included) — checking/reading problems needs the build's own already-installed `node_modules`/`tsc` from a *prior* live-preview install, but not a *currently running* preview, the same reasoning `edit_fn`/`turns_fn` already established. Error mapping: `BuildNotFoundError`→404, `NoWebTargetError`→400 (parallel to `EditNotSupportedError`'s exact semantics), `ToolchainNotInstalledError`→409 (a real resource-state conflict, a status code not otherwise used by this API surface so a client can distinguish "install dependencies first" from every other failure), `ProblemsNotCheckedError`→404 (`GET` only). New control-plane routes `POST`/`GET /jobs/build/{id}/problems`, same generic-proxy shape as every other Job API route, **no credit debit** (a local `tsc` run is not a model call), a new `defaultProblemsTimeout` constant (90s, above ordinary observed single-app compile times, well below `verify/compile.py`'s own much larger internal default of 600s meant for pathological cases this route doesn't need to wait out) on the write route. Gates: agent-engine `task verify` — **Ran 3,625 tests, OK** (21 new: 5 `CheckBuildProblemsTests`, 5 `StudioProblemsStoreTests`, 4 `LiveServeProblemsWiringTests`, 8 new HTTP-level route tests — all against an injected fake `tsc` runner or a fresh tempdir, no real toolchain/subprocess needed in the automated suite); control-plane `go build`/`vet`/`test` all green (45 tests, 7 new); repo `task verify`/`lint`/`security:quick`/`env:check` all pass. Live (real Docker control-plane rebuilt via `control-plane.sh verify`, real agent-engine Studio server in both build-only and preview mode): build-only mode with no toolchain installed → real `409 {"error":"tsc is not installed for this app; run \`pnpm install --ignore-scripts\`... first"}` and a real `404 {"error":"...has not been checked for problems yet"}` before any check — the clear, actionable error this task's design specifically avoids turning into a crash or a silent multi-minute install. Along the way, hit two real, pre-existing environment issues honestly worked through rather than hidden, neither a bug in this task's own code: one preview build hit a real generated-migration collision (`ERROR: relation "bookmark_tag" already exists`, a codegen/migration bug in a many-to-many-heavy generated schema), another's preview page 500'd at runtime — neither blocked the actual test, since a problems check only needs the build's already-installed `node_modules`, not a *working* running preview. Preview mode with a real installed toolchain (confirmed `node_modules/apps/web/node_modules/.bin/tsc` genuinely present on disk) → a real `tsc --noEmit` run surfaced **10 genuine TypeScript errors** in an LLM-synthesized `app/page.tsx` (a missing `@/components/button` module, several not-callable-expression errors on a toast helper, a type mismatch on a `Sidebar` button prop, a missing `Avatar` name, an invalid `AlertVariant` value) — real, substantial, actionable output from the real compiler, not a synthetic fixture, and the same real bug explains why that build's own preview page was 500ing at runtime. Caching confirmed live: a repeated `GET` returned in 12ms, byte-identical to the original `POST` response — the stored report, not a recompile. Unauthenticated calls → `401`; `POST` against an unknown build id → real `404`. NEXT: R-481 (tabbed workspace) — the final task of the approved 7-task roadmap.

2026-09-19  R-479  Console — live preview UI. Fifth of the founder-approved 7-task plan (R-475–R-481). An iframe rendering the real running generated app, wired to R-478's four routes. Preview start is synchronous (confirmed live during R-478's own smoke test), so there's no server-side "starting" state to poll for — polling's real job is **crash detection**, not progress-watching: a 5s interval while `status.status === "ready"`, calling `GET /jobs/preview`, since the agent-engine's own `status()` lazily notices a subprocess that died on its own. New `app/studio/studio-preview.tsx`: one effect triggers `POST /jobs/build/{id}/preview` whenever `buildId` becomes real or a `previewVersion` counter (bumped by `studio-chat.tsx` after every successful edit — `_edit()` never restarts the preview on its own, unlike `_build()`) changes; a second effect runs the 5s crash-detection poll; a third ticks elapsed time while starting so the spinner shows real progress (cold starts confirmed live up to ~45s). A 404 from either preview route sets a `disabled` flag and renders an honest static message instead of a broken iframe. Manual Restart/Stop controls wired to the real R-478 routes, reusing the `RefreshIcon`/`StopIcon` that existed unused since R-475, purpose-built for exactly this. New `lib/control-plane.ts` additions (`PreviewStatus` type — every status shape verified by reading `preview.py` directly before implementation, not assumed — plus `getPreviewStatus`/`stopPreview`/`restartPreview`/`previewBuild` clients) and four new proxy routes (`app/api/preview`, `.../stop`, `.../restart`, `app/api/jobs/build/[id]/preview`). Wired into `studio-chat.tsx` via a new `previewVersion` state, rendered inside a new `.studio-workspace-column` alongside the existing `StudioWorkspace` panel inside `.studio-grid`. A real lint finding surfaced during implementation, not a design change: `eslint-plugin-react-hooks`'s `set-state-in-effect` rule flagged a synchronous `setState` call at the top of the buildId-change effect (the classic React-docs "set loading, then fetch" pattern) — fixed by moving it to be the first statement inside the effect's own async IIFE instead of before it, and consolidating `starting`/`elapsedMs`/`fetchError` into one combined state object so there is exactly one `setState` call there, not several cascading ones. Gates: console `typecheck`/`lint`/`build` all clean (18 routes, 4 new); `task verify` — **3,604 OK** (unchanged, no backend files touched by this task); repo `task verify`/`lint`/`security:quick`/`env:check` all pass. Live (real Docker control-plane + real agent-engine Studio server in preview mode + a freshly rebuilt `next start`): first discovered and cleaned up a real operational gap from R-478's own smoke test (an orphaned generated-app preview process left running because the agent-engine had been killed non-gracefully — a real cleanup gap worth naming, not a bug in this task's code) before starting fresh. Then: a real build immediately triggered the exact `POST /jobs/build/{id}/preview` call `<StudioPreview>`'s effect makes, returning a real `200 {"status":"ready","web_url":...}` whose `web_url`, fetched directly, returned genuine Next.js-rendered HTML — proof the iframe would show a real working app, not a blank frame; a real edit produced a genuine second commit, immediately followed by the exact re-preview call the `previewVersion` bump triggers, landing on a **new** port — confirming fresh, edited code was served, not stale content. The task's most important new behavior, confirmed live: the real OS process behind the current preview port was found and `kill -9`'d directly (a true external crash, not a graceful stop) — the very next poll correctly reported `"stopped"` with the agent-engine's own real message, proving the crash-detection design works. Manual Restart recovered cleanly on a new port; manual Stop worked. Switching the agent-engine to build-only mode made both preview routes return the real, uniform `404 {"error":"preview controls are not enabled"}` through the console proxy — exactly the status `<StudioPreview>` checks for to render the honest disabled message. NEXT: R-480 (backend: Problems/compile-report support) per the approved plan, then R-481 (tabbed workspace).

2026-09-19  R-478  Backend — live preview proxy (local-only). Fourth of the founder-approved 7-task plan (R-475–R-481). Four new control-plane routes proxying the agent-engine's existing trusted-local preview control surface verbatim — correctly **four** routes, not three: `GET /jobs/preview`, `POST /jobs/preview/stop`, `POST /jobs/preview/restart` (the singleton control surface) plus `POST /jobs/build/{id}/preview` (the build-scoped one a chat-per-build UI actually needs, proxying to `POST /api/history/preview` with a **server-constructed** `{"id": id}` body — the caller's own body is never trusted for which build to preview, matching every other build-scoped route in this package). All four auth-required, no credit debit (viewing/controlling a local preview isn't a billable model call). Zero agent-engine changes — every route reused exactly as-is. Generalized `proxyGet` into `proxyUpstream(method, body)`, covering GET and the new credit-free POST control routes alike. A real, verified shape, not assumed: an unknown/evicted build's build-scoped preview route does not 404 — `_preview_recorded_build` has no exception path for that case, it returns a plain `{"status":"error","message":"..."}` dict sent as a real **200**, confirmed by reading the source before writing the tests and then confirmed live too; the proxy deliberately forwards this shape unchanged rather than "fixing" it into a 404. New `defaultPreviewTimeout` constant (60s, above the real 45s internal readiness wait) applied to both `handleBuildPreview` and `handlePreviewRestart` — the latter added during implementation, beyond the original plan sketch, once it was clear `restart()` can itself trigger a real cold start, the same risk build-preview has (confirmed live). Gates: control-plane `go build`/`vet`/`test` all green (38 tests in `internal/jobs`, 13 new, including a dedicated test asserting the 200-with-error-status shape survives the proxy unchanged); repo `task verify`/`lint`/`security:quick`/`env:check` all pass (test count unchanged — no agent-engine or console-web files touched by this task). Live (real Docker control-plane rebuilt via `control-plane.sh verify`, real agent-engine Studio server in preview mode via `studio-preview`): a real build automatically started a real preview as a side effect of the build itself (existing pre-R-478 behavior) — a genuine `pnpm install` plus a real Next.js dev server and a real FastAPI backend, confirmed in the agent-engine's own log output, including a real Groq rate-limit retry and a real JSX-synthesis fallback to the deterministic template along the way (an honest real-world signal, not a bug in this task); `GET /jobs/preview` returned real live local URLs; `POST /jobs/build/1/preview` sent with a deliberately mismatched body proved the server-constructed-body design (a genuine re-preview on new ports, the caller's own body correctly ignored); `POST /jobs/build/nonexistent-999/preview` returned the real 200-with-error-status shape live, confirming the finding above outside of the unit tests too; stop and restart both worked, restart completing a genuine cold start well within the new 60s write-deadline extension; the account's credit balance stayed unchanged (100) across all four preview-control calls, confirming none of them debited anything; switching the agent-engine to build-only mode made all four routes return the identical, uniform real `404 {"error":"preview controls are not enabled"}` — the honest, zero-new-logic passthrough design confirmed. A fresh build afterward succeeded normally, proving the full recovery loop. `GET /studio`'s rendered HTML confirmed the new `studio-grid`/`chat-rail`/`chat-composer` markup present with no server errors. 2026-09-19  R-476  Backend — multi-turn edit bridge. Second of the founder-approved 7-task plan (R-475–R-481). A no-op edit still charges real credits (the model call happened even if the diff was empty) — locked in by a dedicated test, since it's non-obvious and easy to regress. Python side (`studio/live_serve.py`): `_edit()` now threads a real `UsageLedger` through `resolve_generation_provider_from_env`, adding a real `"usage"` key to both its response branches; `_build()` now calls `session_store.record_turn` for its own user/assistant turn. Gates: control-plane `go build`/`vet`/`test` all green (25 tests in `internal/jobs`, 10 new); agent-engine `task verify` — **Ran 3,604 tests, OK**; repo `task verify`/`lint`/`security:quick`/`env:check` all pass. 2026-09-18  R-473  Studio v1 in the console — build an app from the product, not curl. Phase D of `R_&_D/OmniStackAI_Commercial_Platform_Kickoff_v1.md`, first slice: a logged-in user can open `apps/console-web`'s new `/studio` page, type a plain-English app description, click Build, and get a real app built through R-472's real Job API — with the console showing the real post-debit credit balance. Deliberately scoped small rather than the kickoff doc's full Phase D vision (persistent chat + top tabs Preview/Files/Code/Problems/Publish/More + Model Provider settings + a Problems tab): building all of that in one task would repeat a mistake this project's own history explicitly avoids, since the agent-engine's own hybrid-UI engine shipped across four separate, gated Tracker IDs (R-465 synthesis, R-466 compile-repair, R-467 file browser + toggle, R-468 multi-turn edit) rather than one large task. This ships the smallest real, end-to-end slice — prompt in, real build out, real credit debit reflected in the UI — with the file browser, live preview, and chat/multi-turn edit named as follow-ups, not silently dropped. New `app/studio/page.tsx` (Server Component, mirrors `app/page.tsx`'s auth gate exactly — `getCurrentUser()` redirects to `/login` when signed out); new `app/studio/studio-form.tsx` (Client Component: a prompt textarea, a Build button, a real pending state since builds take real time — 3 seconds to 3-plus minutes observed live, both in this task and R-472's — a result panel rendering name/description/entities/file_count/commit_sha/files plus the `usage` block and `credits_spent` when the agent-engine reported them, and an error banner on failure); new `app/api/jobs/build/route.ts` (server-side proxy: reads the session cookie via `getSessionToken()`, 401s immediately with **no** upstream call when it is absent, then forwards the request body to the control-plane's `POST /jobs/build` with the bearer token attached — never exposed to client-side JavaScript, the exact boundary every existing `app/api/auth/*` route already draws; no artificial timeout, since R-472's own `http.ResponseController.SetWriteDeadline` fix is what makes waiting out a real multi-minute build possible in the first place). `lib/control-plane.ts` gained `BuildJobResponse`/`BuildJobUsage`/`buildApp()`, following the exact `callControlPlane` pattern `login`/`registerAccount` already use — only the fields this UI actually renders are typed, with an index signature so other build-kind-specific fields (e.g. `pack_id`) still round-trip without the UI needing to know about them. `app/page.tsx` gained a link to `/studio`; `app/globals.css` gained a `.field textarea` rule (only `input` was styled before) and small `.pill-row`/`.pill`/`.file-list` treatments for the result panel, reusing every existing design token — no new UI dependency. No changes to `services/control-plane` or `services/agent-engine`: R-472 already built the real backend this task's UI calls. Gates: `pnpm run typecheck`/`lint`/`build` all clean (11 routes, with `/studio` and `/api/jobs/build` both correctly dynamic since they read the session cookie per request); `task verify` — **Ran 3,603 tests, OK, Stage 0 verification passed**; `task lint`/`security:quick`/`env:check` all pass; a new `scripts/test.sh` R-473 contract block confirms the new files exist, `buildApp()` is defined, and the build route checks `getSessionToken()` before proxying. **Live verification**: real Docker Compose Postgres+control-plane (`bash scripts/control-plane.sh verify`), a real agent-engine Studio server on local Ollama (`OMNISTACKAI_PREFER_LOCAL=1`, kept free and reproducible matching R-472's precedent), and a real `next start` console proved: `GET /studio` with no session cookie → `307` to `/login`; with a real session cookie → `200`, the server-rendered page showing the correct credit balance; `POST /api/jobs/build` with no cookie → `401 {"error":"not signed in"}` instantly, with no upstream call attempted. Two real build attempts through the console's own new route: the first hit a genuine, unforced local-model IR-validation failure (`request_schema must be a 1-64 char alphanumeric identifier`), which the agent-engine rejected and the control-plane's honest passthrough surfaced as a real `502` with the agent-engine's own error message — an authentic, live proof of the "upstream error renders as a clear error banner" acceptance criterion, not a synthetic test, since it is exactly the shape `studio-form.tsx`'s error-handling path expects. The second attempt (a different prompt) succeeded for real: a genuine 157-file "Recipe Box" repository (`entities: ["Recipe", "User"]`, a real `commit_sha`, a real `files` list, `usage: {cost_micros_usd: 0, total_calls: 2, successful_calls: 1, failed_calls: 1, ...}`, `credits_spent: 0`, `credit_balance: 100`) — correct (local Ollama stays credit-exempt) and an exact field-for-field match for everything `BuildResult` in `studio-form.tsx` renders. Cleanup after the smoke test (killed the console/Studio server processes, `docker compose down`, removed temp response/cookie files) left a clean working tree containing only this task's intended changes, confirmed via `git status --short`. Honest limitation, same as R-471's precedent: verified as thoroughly as possible via curl against the real running server without literally driving a browser — the founder clicking through `/studio` themselves is the final confirmation that client-side rendering and interaction work as designed. NEXT: continue Phase D — port the agent-engine's own `studio/page.py` capabilities (file browser + live preview from R-467, multi-turn chat/edit from R-468) into the console, and/or add Solution Pack/Ecosystem build selection to the Studio UI. Phase E (plan-gated UI, admin console, payment processor) still needs its own explicit founder sign-off before starting.

2026-09-18  R-472  Bridge the control-plane's Job API to the agent-engine — real credit debiting. Phase C of `R_&_D/OmniStackAI_Commercial_Platform_Kickoff_v1.md`: the control-plane's new `POST /jobs/build` authenticates the caller (a new exported `auth.RequireUser`, factored out of `handleMe`), forwards the request body verbatim to `${OMNISTACKAI_AGENT_ENGINE_URL}/api/build`, and — on a successful response reporting real usage — debits the caller's credit balance via a new `users.Store.DebitCredits` (one transaction, row-locked `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE`, clamped via a pure, unit-tested `clampCharge` helper so `credit_balance` never goes negative; v1 policy is to never block a build, only clamp the charge), returning the agent-engine's own JSON body augmented with `credits_spent`/`credit_balance`. Correction found while researching: the Studio's real plain-prompt build path (`intake/provider_resolution.py::resolve_generation_provider_from_env`) returns a **raw** `ModelProvider`, bypassing the `ModelGateway`'s own accounting hook (`UsageLedger`) entirely — that ledger was real and tested already, but until now was only ever exercised by the illustrative `console_snapshot` dashboard, never a real build. Closed with a new, small, additive `model_gateway.RecordingProvider` decorator (new file) that implements the exact same `ModelProvider` protocol and records every real call's usage/cost into an injected ledger — zero changes to any existing provider or call site. `resolve_generation_provider_from_env` gained an optional `usage_ledger` param (default `None` preserves today's exact behavior); `intake/build_app.py::app_build_result_to_dict` gained an additive `usage` key (same pattern R-467 used for `ui_outcomes`); `studio/live_serve.py`'s plain-prompt `_build` creates one `UsageLedger()` per request and surfaces a JSON-safe summary with `cost_micros_usd` as an **int** (`cost_usd * 1_000_000`, rounded), not a decimal string, avoiding any float/precision risk crossing the Go/Python boundary. New env vars `OMNISTACKAI_AGENT_ENGINE_URL` (default `http://127.0.0.1:4173`, containers default to `http://host.docker.internal:4173`) and `OMNISTACKAI_CREDITS_PER_USD` (default `1000`, i.e. 1 credit = $0.001). Two real bugs found and fixed: (1) caught by the new tests before any commit — `RecordingProvider.generate()` initially recorded `provider_id`/`model_id` from the response's own echoed `.model` field rather than `self._inner.provider_id`/`request.model.model_id` (the pattern `ModelGateway._record` actually uses — the truly-dispatched provider, not whatever a response happens to echo); (2) found only by the live smoke test — the control-plane's global 15-second `http.Server.WriteTimeout` (tuned for fast routes like `/auth/*`) was silently killing `/jobs/build`'s connection before a real, multi-minute build could finish; fixed with `http.NewResponseController(w).SetWriteDeadline(...)` scoped to just this one handler rather than loosening the server-wide timeout. Also fixed a Compose networking gap: the agent-engine's Studio server runs on the host (Compose is hard-blocked from adding it as a service), so the containerized control-plane needed `extra_hosts: ["host.docker.internal:host-gateway"]` to reach it — verified reachable from inside the container under Colima before relying on it in the live proof. Chose round-to-nearest credit rounding over this task's own originally-planned ceiling rounding, since ceiling would systematically overcharge every build while the exact real cost stays fully visible in `cost_micros_usd` regardless. Gates: agent-engine `task verify` — **Ran 3,603 tests, OK**, 0 model/network calls (7 new tests in `test_recording_provider.py`, `test_provider_resolution.py`/`test_build_app.py`/`test_studio_server.py` extended); control-plane `go build`/`go vet`/`gofmt` clean, `go test ./...` all green (7 new `internal/jobs` cases covering auth rejection, an internal auth-store error, verbatim body forwarding with a real debit, no-usage no-charge, upstream error passthrough unchanged, an unreachable upstream, and a debit failure; a 7-case `creditsForUsage` table test; a new `internal/users/store_test.go` with a 6-case `clampCharge` table test); repo-wide `task verify`/`lint`/`security:quick`/`env:check` all pass; `go.mod` confirmed still exactly one direct dependency (`pgx`) — the new HTTP client is stdlib-only. **Live verification**: real Docker Compose Postgres+control-plane (`bash scripts/control-plane.sh verify`); a real local Ollama build (forced via `OMNISTACKAI_PREFER_LOCAL=1` to keep this proof free and reproducible, since this machine's real `.env` otherwise defaults to live Groq) through `POST /jobs/build` produced a real 161-file owned "Task Tracker" Git repo with `usage.cost_micros_usd: 0` and `credits_spent: 0` (correct — local usage is credit-exempt by price, not a special case in this task's code); since a genuinely free build has nothing to debit, `DebitCredits`'s row-locked/clamped SQL path was proven separately against the same live Postgres with a small, throwaway, never-committed `go run` program: balance `100 → 63` after `DebitCredits(37)` (persisted, confirmed by a fresh read), then `63 → 0` after an intentional `DebitCredits(999999)` overcharge attempt (clamped, never negative) — independently confirmed via a real `GET /auth/me` through the running API showing `credit_balance: 0`. One small, real, incidental Groq cost was honestly incurred by the very first smoke attempt (before discovering this environment's real cloud default and switching to forced-local) when the model's IR output failed to validate; nothing was charged for that failed build either, since it errored before any usage summary was ever computed. NEXT R-473 (Phase D): rebuild the real Studio/builder UX inside `apps/console-web` so a logged-in user can actually call the now-real `/jobs/build` from the product itself.

2026-09-17  R-471  Fix dev-mode hydration bug + add full name to registration. The founder opened the real R-470 console at `http://127.0.0.1:4321/register` and reported registration silently did nothing. Root cause found in the dev server's own log: `⚠ Blocked cross-origin request to Next.js dev resource /_next/hmr from "127.0.0.1"`, immediately followed by `GET /register?email=...&password=... 200` — Next.js 16 blocks cross-origin access to its own dev/HMR resources by default and treats `127.0.0.1` and `localhost` as distinct origins for that check. With no client JS hydrated, the register form's `onSubmit` handler never attached, so the browser fell back to its native default form submission: a plain GET request with every field serialized into the URL query string, reloading the page and doing nothing, with no visible error to explain why. Fixed with `allowedDevOrigins: ["127.0.0.1", "localhost"]` in `next.config.ts` — exactly the fix Next.js's own warning prescribes. Verified as thoroughly as possible without literally driving a browser: fetched the register page's own actual client JS chunk (taken from the real page HTML, not guessed) with `Origin: http://127.0.0.1:4321` — 200, and the dev server log shows no cross-origin warning this time, where an equivalent request was blocked outright before the fix. Also answered and implemented the founder's question about whether to collect more profile info at registration: added a required **Name** field (`full_name`) — broadly useful for personalization and addressing the user, and the home page previously only had an email to show — via a new, additive migration `000003_users_full_name` (`000001`/`000002` untouched), threaded through `internal/auth`'s `Store` interface, `registerRequest`/`userResponse` types, and `internal/users`' every query that returns a `User`. Explicitly **did not** add Gender or Age: neither serves any function anywhere in this product's actual roadmap (credits, plans, a builder Studio), and collecting them would be unused PII with real privacy/compliance liability (gender is special-category data under some privacy regimes) for zero product benefit — a deliberate decision, not a silent omission. Console changes: `lib/control-plane.ts`'s `ControlPlaneUser`/`registerAccount` gained `name`; `app/api/auth/register/route.ts` validates and forwards it; `app/register/page.tsx` gained a required Name field; `app/page.tsx` now greets "Welcome back, {name}" instead of showing only the email. Renumbered the kickoff doc's Phase C from R-471 to **R-472**, since this task took the R-471 slot as its own real, scoped work rather than being folded silently into R-470 or Phase C. Gates: control-plane `go build`/`go vet`/`gofmt` all clean; `go test ./...` all green (`internal/auth` 15 tests including 2 new sub-cases for a missing and a whitespace-only name; `internal/config` 2; `internal/health` 3; `internal/password` 8; `migrations` 4); console `typecheck`/`lint`/`build` all clean; `task verify` — **Ran 3,593 tests in 62.945s — OK; Stage 0 verification passed**. **Live verification**: rebuilt and restarted the real Docker Compose control-plane (`bash scripts/control-plane.sh verify` — migration `000003` applied cleanly on the pre-existing Postgres volume, re-proving R-469's self-healing migration runner works exactly as designed), then `POST /api/auth/register` with no name → 400; with a name → 201, the name present in the response body; `GET /` with the resulting session cookie → 200, the raw HTML containing "Welcome back, Saurabh Chopra"; a direct `POST http://127.0.0.1:8080/auth/register` bypassing the console entirely confirmed the same round trip against the real Postgres-backed `users.Store`. Honestly noted in `.ai/tasks/R-471.md`: this is the strongest verification of the hydration fix achievable without literally driving a browser — the founder retrying registration themselves is the final confirmation. NEXT R-472 (Phase C): bridge the control-plane's Job API to the unmodified agent-engine so a real generation call actually debits a user's credits (local Ollama usage stays credit-exempt).

2026-09-17  R-470  Real Next.js console-web, wired to R-469's auth API. Phase B of `R_&_D/OmniStackAI_Commercial_Platform_Kickoff_v1.md`: replaced the static, dependency-free `apps/console-web` (plain HTML/CSS/JS reading a committed `data/overview.json` snapshot) with a real Next.js (App Router, TypeScript) app. Register/login/logout wired to R-469's control-plane (`POST /auth/register`, `/auth/login`, `/auth/logout`, `GET /auth/me`) via server-side Route Handlers (`app/api/auth/*`) that proxy the calls and set/clear an `httpOnly`, `sameSite=lax` session cookie — the raw token is never handed to client-side JavaScript and no CORS is needed, since the browser only ever talks to the Next.js origin. `lib/session.ts`'s `getCurrentUser()` reads the cookie server-side and resolves it against the control-plane's `/auth/me` for Server Components. `app/page.tsx` (the authenticated home page) redirects to `/login` if there's no valid session, otherwise renders the profile, plan, and credit balance and a logout control. `app/fabric/page.tsx` carries the old model/cost overview dashboard forward on the exact same `data/overview.json` data contract, now a React Server Component importing the JSON at build time instead of hand-written DOM manipulation. No new UI dependency beyond React/Next.js itself — the existing console's CSS custom properties (indigo accent, light/dark via `prefers-color-scheme`, card/badge/table language) carry forward into `app/globals.css` unchanged in spirit; a real design system is Phase D's job once the actual Studio UX is rebuilt on this foundation. New env var `OMNISTACKAI_CONTROL_PLANE_URL` (server-side only, default `http://127.0.0.1:8080`) is the URL the console calls the control-plane on, kept distinct from the control-plane's own bind address. Old `index.html`/`app.js`/`styles.css` removed (replaced, not left as dead code); `scripts/console.sh` rewritten around `next dev`/`build`/`lint`/`typecheck`/`start` instead of a static `python3 -m http.server`; `Taskfile.yml` gained `console:dev`/`console:lint`/`console:typecheck`/`console:build`/`console:start`; `scripts/verify.sh` gained the two new console gates (`next build` succeeds with no control-plane running, since Route Handlers are compiled, not executed, at build time — keeping `task verify` Docker-free for this slice); `scripts/test.sh` gained a new R-470 contract-assertion block. **Five real ecosystem-compatibility issues found and fixed while implementing** (full detail in `.ai/tasks/R-470.md`'s "Live discovery" section): (1) `pnpm install` timed out fetching `next`/`@next/swc-darwin-arm64` (a raw `curl` of the same tarball took ~50s on this network, right at pnpm's default per-attempt timeout edge) — fixed with a new root `.npmrc` (longer `fetch-timeout`, more retries); (2) `typescript@7.0.2` — genuinely npm's current `latest` — is not yet supported by `typescript-eslint@8.70.0` (`eslint` refused to run at all) — `eslint-config-next@16.3.5`'s own `devDependencies` pin `typescript: 6.0.2`, so the console was pinned to `typescript@6.0.3` (latest 6.x) instead, with `tsc --noEmit` itself working fine under either version (pure ecosystem-tooling lag, not a real TypeScript problem); (3) the documented `FlatCompat` + `compat.extends("next/core-web-vitals", "next/typescript")` ESLint pattern crashed with `TypeError: Converting circular structure to JSON` inside `@eslint/eslintrc`'s error formatter on both `eslint@10.10.0` and `eslint@9.9.1` (never actually an ESLint-version issue) — root cause was that `eslint-config-next@16.3.5`'s default export is already a native flat-config array, so the legacy compat shim is unnecessary for this version and its validator chokes formatting an unrelated error because of a circular self-reference inside `eslint-plugin-react`'s own flat config; fixed by importing `eslint-config-next` directly and dropping `@eslint/eslintrc` entirely; (4) pnpm 11.19 silently stopped reading `package.json`'s `pnpm.*` fields (warned once) — moved `onlyBuiltDependencies`/`allowBuilds` (needed to let `unrs-resolver`'s postinstall script run) to `pnpm-workspace.yaml` instead; (5) **most importantly, the session cookie's `Secure` flag was originally wired from `NODE_ENV === "production"`, which is wrong** — `next start` always sets `NODE_ENV=production` regardless of the real protocol, so every cookie issued locally over plain `http://127.0.0.1` was marked `Secure`, invisible in the first curl-based smoke test (curl does not enforce the `Secure` attribute the way a real browser does), but a real browser refuses to send a `Secure` cookie back over a non-HTTPS origin, which would have silently broken login persistence for anyone actually using the app locally; caught by inspecting the raw `Set-Cookie` header rather than trusting 200/204 status codes alone, and fixed with a new per-request `isSecureRequest()` helper (checks `x-forwarded-proto`, then the request's own `nextUrl.protocol`) used by all three auth routes. Gates: `apps/console-web` `typecheck`/`lint`/`build` all clean; `task verify` — **Ran 3,593 tests in 63.552s — OK; Stage 0 verification passed** (agent-engine and control-plane untouched, the two new console steps run inside it). **Live manual smoke, run twice** (real `next start` on port 4321 + the real, still-running R-469 Docker Compose control-plane/Postgres) — the second run, after the `Secure`-cookie fix, is authoritative: `POST /auth/register` → 201 (`credit_balance: 100`, no `token` field in the body, `Set-Cookie` correct **without** `Secure` over plain HTTP, verified in both the header and curl's own cookie-jar file) → duplicate register, same email → 409 → `GET /` with the cookie → 200, the real registered email rendered (proving the full cookie → control-plane `/auth/me` → rendered-profile round trip) → `GET /fabric` → 200, "Model Fabric" rendered → `GET /` with no cookie → **307 redirect to `/login`** → `POST /auth/logout` → 204, cookie cleared (`Max-Age=0`) → `GET /` with the now-cleared cookie → **307 redirect to `/login`** again (real server-side session invalidation, not just local) → `POST /auth/login` with the same credentials → 200, a **new** session cookie issued. NEXT R-471 (Phase C): bridge the control-plane's Job API to the unmodified agent-engine so a real generation call actually debits a user's credits (local Ollama usage stays credit-exempt).

2026-09-17  R-469  Control-plane foundation — users, auth, plans, credits. Phase A of `R_&_D/OmniStackAI_Commercial_Platform_Kickoff_v1.md`, the founder's decision (2026-09-17) to advance from the Stage-0 static console (`apps/console-web`) and stdlib Studio prototype toward the real commercial platform `OmniStackAI_Implementation_Brief_v6.md` Section 33 has always specified — a Next.js console over a Go control-plane (Auth/Orgs/Billing) and the existing Python agent-engine. Gave the existing `services/control-plane` Go skeleton (health-check-only before this task, one migration that only created `schema_migrations` + enabled `pgvector`) real users, authentication, and a plan/credit model. Two roles only (`super_admin` full access, `user` gated entirely by `plan` — never a second role tier); plans reuse the tier names already named in the Implementation Brief Section 22 (`free`/`developer`/`pro`/`agency`/`enterprise`, `byok` an add-on flag rather than a separate tier); every signup gets the `free` plan plus a starting credit grant (env-configurable via `OMNISTACKAI_SIGNUP_CREDIT_GRANT`, default 100) recorded in an append-only `credit_ledger`. New migration `000002_users_auth_billing` (`users`, `credit_ledger`, `sessions`, transactional, idempotent) plus a new `migrations` package (`//go:embed *.up.sql`) whose `Apply(ctx, pool)` replays every embedded migration, in ascending version order, inside a Go-managed transaction, on every control-plane boot — safe because every migration file is written idempotently, and necessary because `docker-entrypoint-initdb.d` (the pre-existing fast path in `compose.yaml`) only runs against a brand-new empty Postgres volume, never an existing one. Password hashing is PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256 (RFC 8018) implemented directly on `crypto/hmac`+`crypto/sha256`+`crypto/subtle` — **zero new `go.mod` dependency**; `golang.org/x/crypto/bcrypt` was considered and rejected specifically because it would be this repository's first new Go dependency beyond the one already-necessary `pgx` (no stdlib Postgres driver exists, but there is no equivalent excuse for password hashing). New `internal/auth`: `User`/`Store`/`Hasher` interfaces (the same "narrow interface, wire the real implementation at the edge" shape `internal/health`'s `Pinger` already established), `Register(mux, Deps)` mounting `POST /auth/register`, `POST /auth/login`, `POST /auth/logout`, `GET /auth/me`. Login failure is a generic 401 regardless of wrong-password vs. unknown-email — including a same-cost dummy-hash `Verify` call on the "not found" path, computed once per handler construction (not per-request, not a package-level global) from whichever `Hasher` that `Deps` carries, so there is no timing side channel for enumerating registered emails. Opaque `crypto/rand`-generated session bearer tokens; only their SHA-256 hash is ever persisted (`internal/auth/token.go`). New `internal/users`: the real PostgreSQL-backed `Store` (`var _ auth.Store = (*Store)(nil)` compiles); `CreateUser` wraps the user insert and the signup credit-grant ledger row in one database transaction, so a user's `credit_balance` and its ledger history can never disagree. Deliberately **not** unit-tested against a live database in `go test` (keeps `control-plane:test` exactly as hermetic as it already was, matching how `internal/health` tests a fake `Pinger` rather than a real database) — proven instead by a real Docker Compose smoke test. `internal/health`'s `NewHandler` was renamed to `Register(mux, database, pingTimeout)` so `cmd/control-plane/main.go` can mount health and auth routes on one shared `http.ServeMux` (behavior/JSON bodies unchanged). `internal/config` gained two new env-configurable fields (`OMNISTACKAI_SESSION_TTL`, default `720h`; `OMNISTACKAI_SIGNUP_CREDIT_GRANT`, default `100`), following the exact existing `valueOrDefault`/typed-parse pattern. `main.go` now calls `migrations.Apply` before serving traffic and wires the real `users.Store` plus a `password`-backed `auth.Hasher` adapter. `Dockerfile` gained `COPY migrations ./migrations` (required for the new `//go:embed` to see its files during the container build); `compose.yaml` gained a second `docker-entrypoint-initdb.d` mount for `000002` and the two new env vars; `.env.example`/`scripts/env-check.sh` document and enforce them; `scripts/test.sh` gained a new R-469 contract-assertion block (matching the existing per-Tracker-ID convention) checking the new files exist, the migration creates the three new tables, all four auth routes are present, and — the concrete, checkable proof password hashing stayed stdlib-only — `go.mod`'s direct-dependency count is still exactly 1 (`pgx`). **Live discovery while implementing:** an initial design for the login-timing mitigation used a package-level mutable global (`SetDefaultHasher`) plus a `sync.Once`-cached dummy hash — this had a real footgun (a panic on the very first login if `main.go` forgot to call `SetDefaultHasher`, plus stale-cache risk across tests using different fake hashers). Replaced with computing the dummy hash once per handler construction from the request's own injected `Hasher`, removing the global entirely — simpler, no initialization-order dependency. Tests: `migrations_test.go` (4), `password_test.go` (8, including one real-default-iteration-count case and fast lower-iteration cases for the rest), `token_test.go` + `handler_test.go` in `internal/auth` (15, against an in-memory fake `Store`/`Hasher` — register/login/logout/me happy paths, duplicate email, wrong password vs. unknown email returning byte-identical error bodies, missing/expired/garbage bearer tokens, idempotent logout), `config_test.go` extended (2 tests, 9 subtests). Gates: `go test ./...` all green; `control-plane` lint/build passed; `scripts/test.sh`'s new block passed; `task verify` — **Ran 3,593 tests in 62.421s — OK; Stage 0 verification passed** (agent-engine untouched, 0 model/network calls, no slowdown); lint/security/env green. **Live manual smoke** (Colima started, real Docker Compose + real PostgreSQL): the control-plane image built successfully with the new `COPY migrations` layer, both containers reported `Healthy`, the live readiness check passed (proving `migrations.Apply` ran cleanly against the real database on boot); a full `curl` round trip against the running container proved: register (201, `role":"user"`, `"plan":"free"`, `"credit_balance":100`, a real token) → duplicate register (409) → login with the correct password (200, a *different* new token) → login with the wrong password (401) → login with an unknown email (401, a **byte-identical** error body to the wrong-password case — no user-enumeration leak in production, not just in the fake-store unit tests) → `GET /auth/me` authenticated (200, correct profile, no token echoed) → `GET /auth/me` with no `Authorization` header (401) → logout (204) → `GET /auth/me` with the now-invalid token (401, "session not found or expired" — the session was actually deleted from real PostgreSQL, not just marked in memory) → logout again on the already-invalid token (204, idempotent as designed). `apps/console-web` and `services/agent-engine` untouched — `studio/page.py` remains the agent-engine's own local proof harness, not the commercial product UI. NEXT R-470 (Phase B): replace the static `apps/console-web` with a real Next.js (App Router, TypeScript) app wired to these four auth endpoints — the point where `task bootstrap`/`task doctor` deliberately gain a real Node/npm toolchain requirement for the console, a scope change already recorded in `R_&_D/OmniStackAI_Commercial_Platform_Kickoff_v1.md`.

2026-09-17  R-468  Multi-turn chat / "continue editing this app" in the Studio — every `/api/build` call used to be a fresh, stateless one-shot prompt, with no way to say "now add a favorites feature to the app I just built" and have it apply as a further edit to the same repo. Two rounds of direct-code investigation confirmed no session/conversation concept existed anywhere in the repo, that the apply/commit half already worked end-to-end and tested (`edit/diff.py::plan_edit` → `assemble_project` + `diff_projects`, generic over any two `ApplicationIR`s; `edit/apply.py::commit_edit` → `apply_diff` + `git_service.commit_all`, proven by `tests/test_edit_loop.py::CommitEditTests::test_commit_edit_records_a_second_commit`), and that the missing half — producing a second IR from a follow-up prompt — had no existing code. Rejected the obvious alternative (re-calling `generate_ir` with an augmented prompt and diffing old vs new) for lack of any guarantee the model preserves existing entities/screens verbatim (the intake system prompt actively says to "replace the template content"); chose instead a generic, validated delta mirroring `solution_packs/ai_delta.py`'s proven shape. New `intake/app_delta.py`: `AppDeltaProposal(entities=(), apis=(), screens=(), rationale="")` (the same bounded/unique validation as `AIDeltaProposal` minus pack-specific fields); `build_app_delta_messages(base_ir, follow_up_prompt)` lists the app's real existing entities/apis/screens/roles as context and instructs the model to propose only what's new; `parse_app_delta_proposal(text, base_ir=...)` strictly parses untrusted model JSON, rejecting any collision with the base IR (entity name, api method+path, screen id) at parse time; `apply_app_delta(base_ir, proposal)` independently re-checks every collision (defense in depth, mirroring `solution_packs/application.py:196-231`'s merge exactly) before merging by tuple concatenation (`base_ir.entities + proposal.entities` via `dataclasses.replace`), backstopped by `ApplicationIR.__post_init__` and the same `validate_ir`/`has_errors` semantic check; `generate_app_delta_proposal(...)` is a bounded validate→feedback→retry loop (default 3 attempts, retry only on a validation rejection — never on a provider exception, mirroring R-465's `_synthesize_file` idiom). **Zero changes to `edit/diff.py`, `edit/apply.py`, `edit/patch.py`, `edit/errors.py`, `git_service/`, or `application_ir/`** — every diff/commit primitive is reused exactly as-is. New `studio/session.py`: `StudioSessionStore` — bounded (`OrderedDict`-based LRU-by-last-touched eviction), thread-safe, in-memory, server-only, keyed by the same build id `StudioBuildHistory` assigns; `begin`/`get`/`advance`/`record_turn`/`turns_view` (the one JSON-safe, secret-free view exposed over HTTP — the `ApplicationIR` itself is never sent to the browser); `EditNotSupportedError` for build kinds this mechanism doesn't support. `studio/live_serve.py`'s `_build` now sets a local `editable_ir` and starts a session (`session_store.begin`) only for the two build kinds that produce exactly one `ApplicationIR` — plain-prompt and single-surface Ecosystem Pack builds; Solution Pack and "all surfaces" ecosystem builds never get one. New `async _edit(build_id, prompt, *, history, session_store)`: resolve the session (typed not-found) → reject Solution Pack/all-surfaces builds honestly (`EditNotSupportedError`, checked via the recorded history entry's `pack_id`/`is_ecosystem` fields) → resolve a provider (`resolve_generation_provider_from_env`, mocked in every new test from the start — closing the exact class of live-network gap found in R-467) → `generate_app_delta_proposal` → `apply_app_delta` → `plan_edit` (an empty diff returns early with no commit) → `commit_edit` (one new commit on the existing repo, message `edit: <prompt>`) → `session_store.advance` + `record_turn` (a user turn and a short assistant summary from `diff.summary()`) → `history.update(...)` (new method on `StudioBuildHistory`, refreshing `file_count`/`commit_sha`/`entities` on the existing "Recent builds" entry in place — identity/location fields stay fixed) → returns the updated entities/file count/commit sha/diff/rationale/turns. `studio/server.py`: `POST /api/build/{id}/edit` and `GET /api/build/{id}/turns`, via the same `_build_id_for_suffix` path-matching helper R-467 built for the file routes; typed-error → status mapping (not-found→404, unsupported-build-kind→400, other→502, the existing convention); both wired unconditionally in `main()` (editing needs no toolchain, only git and a model — available in build-only mode too). `page.py`: a "Continue editing this app" panel (turn list + text input + "Apply change" button) under the file browser; a successful edit re-renders the entities/file-count/commit fields and refreshes the file browser (files changed); zero external assets preserved (verified with `node --check` on the extracted `<script>` block plus the existing no-CDN/no-`<link>` checks). **Found and fixed while implementing, via the end-to-end test suite (not a live run):** neither this new module nor `solution_packs/ai_delta.py` (the pattern it mirrors) validated that a proposed screen's `role` refers to one of the base IR's *actual* declared roles — only that it was well-formed lower_snake_case. `ApplicationIR.__post_init__` does catch an invalid role as a last resort (it did, in the first failing test run: `minimal-blog-ecosystem`'s "author-studio" surface only declares an `author` role, unlike `minimal-blog`'s `author`+`reader`), but only *after* `generate_app_delta_proposal` had already returned successfully — too late for the retry-with-feedback loop to use it. Fixed by adding an explicit role-existence check to `parse_app_delta_proposal` (same layer as every other base-IR collision check, gets the retry benefit) and to `apply_app_delta` (defense in depth, matching the existing pattern), plus listing the app's real roles in the prompt. Tests: new `tests/test_app_delta.py` (27), `tests/test_studio_session.py` (13), `tests/test_studio_edit.py` (11, end-to-end against real temporary git repos with a stub provider — a real second commit with the new entity's files actually present in the working tree; a second edit stacking a third commit while keeping the first edit's entity; a collision rejected with zero commits made and state unchanged; `StudioBuildHistory`'s entry updated in place, not appended; a provider exception never retried and making no commit; both excluded build kinds returning the honest `EditNotSupportedError`), plus route/page-control additions to `tests/test_studio_server.py` and a round-trip test in `tests/test_studio_history.py`. Gates: focused 214 passed; `task verify` 3,593 OK (0 model/network calls; completed in 63.8s — no slowdown versus before, corroborating no hidden calls were introduced); lint/security/env green; demos clean (359/353 files). Manual smoke (opt-in, real HTTP against a running `live_serve` instance, build-only mode, no live model call — today's Groq daily quota was already exhausted per R-466/R-467): `/healthz` ok; an unknown build's `/turns` → `{"turns": []}` (200, an honest empty view, not an error); an unknown build's `/edit` → 404; a build id containing a slash → 404 (not matched, same convention as the R-467 file routes); the page's HTML contains "Apply change". The core delta→merge→diff→commit round trip was already proven end-to-end by the test suite above, so no further live model call was spent. NEXT R-469: wire R-466's toolchain-dependent `compile_and_repair` into the edit flow (so an edited hybrid-UI screen also gets type-checked/repaired), and/or an undo/revert UI over the real git history every edited build now has on disk.

2026-09-17  R-466  Compile-Level Repair for LLM-Written UI + Rate-Limit-Aware Pacing — the second brick of the hybrid engine, driven by what the R-465/R-466 live runs proved. (1) The model gateway can now pace itself: `ProviderHTTPError` carries `status_code` and `retry_after_seconds`; a typed `ProviderRateLimitedError` (HTTP 429); pure `parse_retry_after` reads the standard `Retry-After` header (delay-seconds or HTTP-date) and falls back to the "Please try again in 6.495s" phrase Groq puts in the body; one `_http_error` builder serves the JSON and stream paths (bounded body, never the key); the cloud adapter's `generate()` waits the hint (+0.5s; 2s/4s backoff without one) and re-sends the SAME request, bounded by `OMNISTACKAI_RATE_LIMIT_RETRIES` (default 2) and `OMNISTACKAI_MAX_RETRY_AFTER_SECONDS` (default 60; a longer ask fails fast), read by the bootstrap and documented in `.env.example`, every wait/give-up logged, injectable sleep; no other error is retried and routing/breaker semantics are unchanged. (2) The compiler has the last word: `verify/compile.py` adds `CompileError`/`CompileReport` (JSON-safe, grouped by file), `parse_tsc_output` (`tsc --noEmit --pretty false`, de-duplicated, POSIX paths), `compile_web_project` (the app's own `node_modules/.bin/tsc`, injectable runner, timeout → `VerifyError`) and `ensure_web_dependencies` (reuse / symlink an existing install / `pnpm install --ignore-scripts`); `codegen/hybrid_repair.py` adds `llm_file_specs` (the exact R-465 prompts, template fallbacks and compact prompts for `app/page.tsx` + `app/<screen>/page.tsx`), `compile_errors_message` (bounded), `repair_compiled_files` (each LLM-written file's compiler errors fed back through R-465's `_repair_message` channel as `[SYSTEM, USER(prompt), ASSISTANT(current file), USER(REJECTED: TypeScript reported N error(s) …)]`, validator-gated, marked `compile-repair k/N`, template fallback, a `UiSynthesisOutcome` per file, and a path without a spec — a deterministic file — is never rewritten), `build_repair_diff` (a `ProjectDiff` of `MODIFIED apps/web/<path>` for `edit/apply_diff`) and `compile_and_repair(_sync)` (compile → repair → apply → recompile for `max_rounds`, reverting still-failing LLM files on the last round → `CompileRepairReport(rounds, repaired, reverted, untouched_failures, final_ok)`). (3) Amendment from the live runs: `codegen/llm_ui.py` gained a `_Transcript` that shrinks when a provider rejects a request as too large (HTTP 413, or a 400 that says "context length"/"too large") — first dropping the echoed prior output (corrective text merged into the prompt turn), then switching to `compact_grounding(ir)` in `nextjs.py` (`summarize_data_layer(max_chars=5_000)`, `summarize_components(max_chars=4_000, max_names_per_file=1)`, `summarize_design_tokens(max_chars=1_000)`; compact prompts ≤ 14k chars, tested) — threaded through the synthesis entry points, the adapter (computed only with a provider; default bytes unchanged), `LlmFileSpec.compact_prompt` and `_repair_one`; `last_reason` now carries the HTTP status of a failed call (`ProviderHTTPError(413)`), never the body. (4) CLI `task agent-engine:ui:synthesize` Step 3/3: link (`OMNISTACKAI_WEB_NODE_MODULES`) or install `node_modules`, compile, repair, revert, commit the repair as the customer identity, print each compile round, repaired/reverted/untouched files and the verdict; skips cleanly without the toolchain. Tests: `test_cloud_rate_limit.py` (15), `test_compile_report.py` (11), `test_hybrid_repair.py` (12), `test_llm_ui_compact.py` (10) — stub providers, fake runners, temp dirs, 0 network. Gates: focused 115 passed; `task verify` 3,490 OK (Stage 0; 0 model calls); lint/security/env green; demos clean; `web-typecheck` PASSED for both examples. Live runs with the founder's Groq key (free tier), reported as found: the three-step CLI ran end-to-end in 13 s (intake OK; all five UI calls failed instantly on a non-429 status; pages fell back; Step 3 linked `node_modules` and compiled at 0 errors, PASSED); a direct probe with the full 19k-char grounded prompt succeeded on an empty window (5,024 in / 3,639 out); a reproduction showed the 429 path working (typed; `Retry-After: 112` honoured) but giving up above the 60 s cap — the limiter was tokens per day (`Limit 200000, Used 191262`), so the daily budget was spent. Pacing and status-carrying outcomes are live-verified; compile repair with real `tsc` errors and shrink-on-413 are stub-verified only. NEXT R-467: product-UI shell v1.

2026-09-17  R-465  Grounded Hybrid UI Synthesis — the first brick of the founder-approved HYBRID generation engine: an LLM writes the modern Next.js UI, constrained to OmniStackAI's deterministic, typed data layer and its design-token layer, with a bounded validation→feedback→retry loop and a deterministic-template fallback, so generated apps are both modern and verifiably wired to a real API + DB + auth. The R-462 seed hallucinated because its prompt hand-described the hooks and had drifted from the generator (`refresh()` vs the real `refetch()`, `page/pageSize` params vs the real `limit/offset`, missing `use<Entity>`/`useUpdate`/`useDelete`/`useList<Child>By<Rel>`, `useAuth` advertised without an auth-provider), told the model to hardcode hex colours despite `styles/tokens.css`, gated per-screen synthesis behind a never-set env var (`OMNISTACKAI_SYNTHESIZE_ALL_SCREENS`), discarded the validator's rejection reason after one attempt, and had a multi-line import bypass. `codegen/nextjs.py` gained `summarize_data_layer(ir)` (parses the REAL generated `lib/types.ts`/`lib/hooks.ts`/`lib/api.ts` — entity interfaces, `Use*` interfaces, every exported hook signature incl. mutation hooks rendered from their bodies, `api.*` names; an explicit "NO data hooks" line for IRs without entity-schema APIs), `_component_files(ir)` (the ~110 component files factored out of `generate()`, byte-identical output) + `summarize_components(ir)` (real export names; auth-provider first and only when `needs_auth`), and `summarize_design_tokens()` (real token names), computed once per project when a provider is present. `codegen/llm_ui.py` was rewritten around one `_synthesize_file` core: `[SYSTEM, USER]`; on validator rejection append the prior output (ASSISTANT, capped 8k chars) + a corrective USER turn carrying the reason and retry up to 3 times; retry ONLY on validator rejection, NEVER on exceptions; exhaustion/exception → the deterministic template with no marker; success marker `// [OmniStackAI] Mode: LLM-Synthesized Bespoke UI (<model>; attempt k/N)`; a JSON-safe, secret-free `UiSynthesisOutcome(path, mode, attempts, model_id, last_reason)` per file; `clean_and_validate_jsx` now scans every static import statement (multi-line aware), allows exactly `react`/`react-dom` plus platform prefixes, and rejects `react-*`, `require(`, dynamic `import(`; the prompt builders accept `data_layer`/`components`/`design_tokens` (lazily computed — no import cycle) and share `_grounding_blocks` + `_core_rules` (token-based styling; the `useAuth` rule conditional on `needs_auth`). An explicit `synthesize_screens: bool = False` + `ui_outcomes` are threaded `NextjsWebAdapter.generate → assemble_project → build_app_from_ir / build_app_from_prompt` (the env gate and the unused `import os` removed; `ModelProvider` gets a `TYPE_CHECKING` import). New opt-in CLI `task agent-engine:ui:synthesize -- "<prompt>"` (`intake/ui_synthesize_run.py`; provider via `OMNISTACKAI_CLOUD_PROVIDER`, Ollama fallback; per-file outcome table; clean bounded exit on provider errors) + `docs/HYBRID_UI.md` + COMMANDS.md. An omitted empty relation collection normalizes safely to `()`, but substantive invalid data fails closed—no fallback is presented as refined. R-431's `surface_to_ir`/`plan_ecosystem` accept an optional validated entity tuple without changing existing callers; `plan_refined_ecosystem` reuses the deterministic CRUD/IR derivation so resulting apps are `validate_ir`-clean and repository wired.
